Like I said, it's just kind of that same empty feeling no matter what I'm doing, so I don't really know.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to enjoy things at all anymore?\n\nPatient: I mean... like I was just saying, I'm really not sure. I guess if I had to give you an answer... probably not? \n\nIt's just hard to tell because everything feels so flat all the time. I don't really have moments anymore where I stop and think, 'oh, I'm having a really good time right now.' It's more like I'm just... there. I just do what I need to do for the day, but I don't think I'm actually enjoying any of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or worried?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I wouldn't say I feel anxious or worried about things. It's not like I'm sitting around stressing or panicking. It's just... more of that heavy, flat feeling I was talking about. I don't really get worked up enough to feel worried.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep patterns?\n\nPatient: Yes, actually. It's been pretty frustrating. I mean, I'll be completely exhausted after standing on the production floor all day for work, but then when I finally get in bed... my mind just keeps running. I just lay there thinking about everything I'm behind on. And even if I do manage to fall asleep, I wake up feeling like I barely rested at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite?\n\nPatient: Yeah, there's been some changes. Most days I just... I don't really have an appetite. I'll end up skipping meals until my shift is almost over, just because nothing sounds good to eat. But then other times, I'm just so drained that I'll grab whatever snacks are in the vending machine late at night. It's messed with my weight a bit... it goes up and down enough that I've noticed my work pants fit differently from month to month.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're moving or speaking more slowly than you used to?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. I mean... I feel exhausted and heavy, like I said, but I don't think I'm actually moving or talking in slow motion. I still have to keep up my normal pace inspecting things on the floor at work, and no one has told me I'm falling behind or talking differently. So, no, I don't think so.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about ending your life?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I mean, I feel really drained and down a lot of the time, but I've never actually thought about... doing that. I just want to feel better, not end things.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. From the dialogue so far and your current candidates, ask the one follow-up question that best separates them.\n\n[Current Turn]\n16\n\n[Current Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1, F32.2, F32.3\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I'm here because... well, for the last couple of years, I just feel low all the time. It's like, even when I'm at work doing my inspections, or at church, or just around people I really care about, there's this heavy, empty feeling. And it just doesn't really lift.\n\nDoctor: Do you find pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I mean... I haven't really noticed, to be honest. I guess I just haven't paid attention to whether I'm actually enjoying things or not lately. Like I said, it's just kind of that same empty feeling no matter what I'm doing, so I don't really know.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to enjoy things at all anymore?\n\nPatient: I mean... like I was just saying, I'm really not sure. I guess if I had to give you an answer... probably not? \n\nIt's just hard to tell because everything feels so flat all the time. I don't really have moments anymore where I stop and think, 'oh, I'm having a really good time right now.' It's more like I'm just... there. I just do what I need to do for the day, but I don't think I'm actually enjoying any of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or worried?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I wouldn't say I feel anxious or worried about things. It's not like I'm sitting around stressing or panicking. It's just... more of that heavy, flat feeling I was talking about. I don't really get worked up enough to feel worried.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep patterns?\n\nPatient: Yes, actually. It's been pretty frustrating. I mean, I'll be completely exhausted after standing on the production floor all day for work, but then when I finally get in bed... my mind just keeps running. I just lay there thinking about everything I'm behind on. And even if I do manage to fall asleep, I wake up feeling like I barely rested at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite?\n\nPatient: Yeah, there's been some changes. Most days I just... I don't really have an appetite. I'll end up skipping meals until my shift is almost over, just because nothing sounds good to eat. But then other times, I'm just so drained that I'll grab whatever snacks are in the vending machine late at night. It's messed with my weight a bit... it goes up and down enough that I've noticed my work pants fit differently from month to month.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're moving or speaking more slowly than you used to?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. I mean... I feel exhausted and heavy, like I said, but I don't think I'm actually moving or talking in slow motion. I still have to keep up my normal pace inspecting things on the floor at work, and no one has told me I'm falling behind or talking differently. So, no, I don't think so.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about ending your life?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I mean, I feel really drained and down a lot of the time, but I've never actually thought about... doing that. I just want to feel better, not end things.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this heavy, empty feeling?\n\nPatient: It's been going on for a while now. Um... I'd say about two years and four months. Yeah, it started right around the time I had to stop taking my community-college classes. I just couldn't afford it anymore, and my work schedule was getting in the way, so I had to drop out. It's pretty much been like this ever since then.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of even a few days where you felt significantly better than you do right now?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's just been... a constant thing. I mean, maybe I'll have a day where work isn't totally awful, but the heavy feeling is always there in the background. I haven't had a stretch of days where I actually felt good or like my old self since this all started.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where your low mood was worse than it is now?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. It hasn't really... dipped into anything worse than this. It's just been this same, steady heavy feeling the whole time. I haven't noticed any specific periods where it got much worse, it's pretty much always just as bad as it is right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any times when you felt restless or couldn't sit still?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I don't think I've had any times where I felt restless or like I couldn't sit still. It's actually kind of the opposite... I mostly just sit there. So, no, I haven't noticed anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight over the past couple of years?\n\nPatient: Well, it's actually been kind of up and down. Most days I just don't have an appetite, so I'll skip eating until my shift is almost over because nothing sounds good. But then sometimes I'm just so drained that I end up grabbing whatever snacks are in the vending machine late at night. Because of that, my weight fluctuates quite a bit... I've noticed my work pants fit differently from month to month.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been fidgeting more than usual?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. Like I said, I'm usually just sitting there. I don't really have the energy to fidget or move around much.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like you're unable to initiate or continue activities due to feeling heavy or sluggish?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. It feels like even the small stuff takes way more energy than I actually have. Like... just filling out my inspection logs at work, or driving to basketball, or even washing my hair. It all just takes so much effort to do. And resting on my days off doesn't really refill me, you know? I just stay drained.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. It's like, even when I'm at work doing my inspections, or at church, or just around people I really care about, there's this heavy, empty feeling. And it just doesn't really lift.\n\nDoctor: Do you find pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I mean... I haven't really noticed, to be honest. I guess I just haven't paid attention to whether I'm actually enjoying things or not lately. Like I said, it's just kind of that same empty feeling no matter what I'm doing, so I don't really know.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to enjoy things at all anymore?\n\nPatient: I mean... like I was just saying, I'm really not sure. I guess if I had to give you an answer... probably not? \n\nIt's just hard to tell because everything feels so flat all the time. I don't really have moments anymore where I stop and think, 'oh, I'm having a really good time right now.' It's more like I'm just... there. I just do what I need to do for the day, but I don't think I'm actually enjoying any of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or worried?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I wouldn't say I feel anxious or worried about things. It's not like I'm sitting around stressing or panicking. It's just... more of that heavy, flat feeling I was talking about. I don't really get worked up enough to feel worried.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep patterns?\n\nPatient: Yes, actually. It's been pretty frustrating. I mean, I'll be completely exhausted after standing on the production floor all day for work, but then when I finally get in bed... my mind just keeps running. I just lay there thinking about everything I'm behind on. And even if I do manage to fall asleep, I wake up feeling like I barely rested at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite?\n\nPatient: Yeah, there's been some changes. Most days I just... I don't really have an appetite. I'll end up skipping meals until my shift is almost over, just because nothing sounds good to eat. But then other times, I'm just so drained that I'll grab whatever snacks are in the vending machine late at night. It's messed with my weight a bit... it goes up and down enough that I've noticed my work pants fit differently from month to month.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're moving or speaking more slowly than you used to?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. I mean... I feel exhausted and heavy, like I said, but I don't think I'm actually moving or talking in slow motion. I still have to keep up my normal pace inspecting things on the floor at work, and no one has told me I'm falling behind or talking differently. So, no, I don't think so.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about ending your life?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I mean, I feel really drained and down a lot of the time, but I've never actually thought about... doing that. I just want to feel better, not end things.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this heavy, empty feeling?\n\nPatient: It's been going on for a while now. Um... I'd say about two years and four months. Yeah, it started right around the time I had to stop taking my community-college classes. I just couldn't afford it anymore, and my work schedule was getting in the way, so I had to drop out. It's pretty much been like this ever since then.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of even a few days where you felt significantly better than you do right now?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's just been... a constant thing. I mean, maybe I'll have a day where work isn't totally awful, but the heavy feeling is always there in the background. I haven't had a stretch of days where I actually felt good or like my old self since this all started.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where your low mood was worse than it is now?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. It hasn't really... dipped into anything worse than this. It's just been this same, steady heavy feeling the whole time. I've noticed my work pants fit differently from month to month.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been fidgeting more than usual?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. Like I said, I'm usually just sitting there. So, no, I don't think so.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about ending your life?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I mean, I feel really drained and down a lot of the time, but I've never actually thought about... doing that. I just want to feel better, not end things.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this heavy, empty feeling?\n\nPatient: It's been going on for a while now. Um... I'd say about two years and four months. Yeah, it started right around the time I had to stop taking my community-college classes. I just couldn't afford it anymore, and my work schedule was getting in the way, so I had to drop out. It's pretty much been like this ever since then.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of even a few days where you felt significantly better than you do right now?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's just been... a constant thing. I mean, maybe I'll have a day where work isn't totally awful, but the heavy feeling is always there in the background. I haven't had a stretch of days where I actually felt good or like my old self since this all started.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where your low mood was worse than it is now?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. It hasn't really... dipped into anything worse than this. It's just been this same, steady heavy feeling the whole time. I haven't noticed any specific periods where it got much worse, it's pretty much always just as bad as it is right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any times when you felt restless or couldn't sit still?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I don't think I've had any times where I felt restless or like I couldn't sit still. It's actually kind of the opposite... I mostly just sit there. So, no, I haven't noticed anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight over the past couple of years?\n\nPatient: Well, it's actually been kind of up and down. Most days I just don't have an appetite, so I'll skip eating until my shift is almost over because nothing sounds good. But then sometimes I'm just so drained that I end up grabbing whatever snacks are in the vending machine late at night. Because of that, my weight fluctuates quite a bit... I've noticed my work pants fit differently from month to month.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been fidgeting more than usual?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. Like I said, I'm usually just sitting there. I don't really have the energy to fidget or move around much.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like you're unable to initiate or continue activities due to feeling heavy or sluggish?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. It feels like even the small stuff takes way more energy than I actually have. Like... just filling out my inspection logs at work, or driving to basketball, or even washing my hair. It all just takes so much effort to do. And resting on my days off doesn't really refill me, you know? I just stay drained.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling irritable lately?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I haven't noticed feeling irritable or snappy with anyone. Honestly, I think I'm just too tired most of the time to get worked up over things.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that this heavy, empty feeling is something that interferes with your daily life most of the time, or is it more of a mild feeling that you can still work around?\n\nPatient: It definitely interferes most of the time. It's not really something I can just work around anymore. I mean... I've actually gotten written warnings at work lately because I keep missing shifts and turning in my inspection documentation late. And it's the same outside of work, too. I've mostly stopped showing up for basketball and dance nights. I even stopped going to the church STEM mentoring program that I used to really care about. So, yeah, it pretty much affects everything I do.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your movements or speech have been slower than they used to be, even if it's just a little bit?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I'm tired all the time, but I haven't noticed my speech or how I move actually slowing down. I haven't really had anyone point anything like that out to me, either.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to care about?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I still care about the things I used to do, like the mentoring program and basketball. I still want to be involved in them. It's just... I don't have the energy to actually go anymore. So the interest is definitely still there, I'm just too drained to act on it.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe your heavy, empty feeling as mild, moderate, or severe most of the time?\n\nPatient: I would say it's severe. It's just really heavy and it's there almost all the time.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
